Ethnic origins in Philadelphia

### Immigration and cultural diversity



Philadelphia's famed [Italian Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Market) in [South Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Philadelphia)



[Barbara Gittings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Gittings) Way, displaying [Gayborhood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_culture_in_Philadelphia) [street sign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainbow_flag_\(LGBT_movement\))

In addition to the city's economic growth, the city's population has been fueled by foreign immigration. According to [The Pew Charitable Trusts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pew_Charitable_Trusts), the city's [foreign-born](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign-born) population increased by 69% between 2000 and 2016 to constitute nearly 20% of Philadelphia's workforce,[^cite_note-Katz2018-75] and it doubled between 1990 and 2017 to constitute 13.8% of the city's total population, with the top five countries of origin being China by a significant margin followed by the Dominican Republic, Jamaica, India, and Vietnam.[^cite_note-133]

| Country | Population |
| --- | --- |
| China | 22,140 |
| Dominican Republic | 13,792 |
| Jamaica | 13,500 |
| India | 11,382 |
| Vietnam | 10,132 |
| Haiti | 9,186 |
| Mexico | 7,823 |
| Ukraine | 6,898 |
| Albania | 5,258 |
| Korea/ North Korea | 4,385 |

Irish, Italian, German, Polish, English, Russian, Ukrainian, and French ancestries constitute the largest [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_emigration) ethnic groups in the city.[^cite_note-135] Philadelphia has the second-largest Irish and Italian populations in the United States after New York City. [South Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Philadelphia) remains one of the largest [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian-American) neighborhoods in the country and is home to the [Italian Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Market).

The [Pennsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsport) neighborhood and [Gray's Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray's_Ferry) section of South Philadelphia, home to many [Mummer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mummers_Parade) clubs, are well known as [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish-American) neighborhoods. The [Kensington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kensington,_Philadelphia), [Port Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Richmond,_Philadelphia), and [Fishtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishtown,_Philadelphia) neighborhoods have historically been heavily Irish and Polish. Port Richmond is a center for the Polish-American community in Philadelphia, and it remains a common destination for Polish immigrants. [Northeast Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Philadelphia), although known for its Irish and Irish-American population, is home to a Jewish and Russian population. [Mount Airy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Airy,_Philadelphia) in [Northwest Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Philadelphia) also contains a Jewish community. Nearby [Chestnut Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chestnut_Hill,_Philadelphia) is historically known as an [Anglo-Saxon Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Anglo-Saxon_Protestant) community.

Philadelphia's [Black American](2154.md) population is the fourth-largest in the country after New York City, Chicago, and [Houston](13774.md). [West Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Philadelphia) and [North Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Philadelphia) are largely African-American neighborhoods, but many are leaving those areas in favor of the Northeast and Southwest sections of Philadelphia. A higher proportion of [African-American Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African-American_Muslims) reside in Philadelphia than most other major U.S. cities. West Philadelphia and [Southwest Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Philadelphia) are home to various [Afro-Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indian_Americans) and [African immigrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_immigration_to_the_United_States) communities.[^cite_note-136]

The [Puerto Rican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Ricans_in_Philadelphia) population in Philadelphia is the second-largest on the U.S. mainland after New York City, and the second-fastest growing after [Orlando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando,_Florida).[^cite_note-137] Eastern North Philadelphia, particularly [Fairhill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairhill) and surrounding areas to the north and east, has one of the highest concentrations of Puerto Ricans outside Puerto Rico, with many large swaths of blocks being close to 100% Puerto Rican.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] Puerto Rican and [Dominican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Americans) populations reside in [North Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Philadelphia) and the Northeast, and [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_American) and Central American populations exist in South Philadelphia.[^cite_note-140] South American migrants were being transported by bus from [Texas](29810.md) to Philadelphia beginning in 2022.[^cite_note-141]

Philadelphia's [Asian American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American) population includes those of Chinese, Indians, Vietnamese, South Koreans, Filipinos, Cambodians, and Indonesians. Over 35,000 Chinese Americans lived in the city in 2015,[^cite_note-142] including a [Fuzhounese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzhounese_Americans) population. Center City hosts a [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Philadelphia) that is served by [Chinatown bus lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown_bus_lines) with service to/from [Chinatown, Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Manhattan).[^cite_note-Katz2018-75] [Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indians_in_the_Philadelphia_metropolitan_area) make up the second-largest Asian group in the city of Philadelphia,[^cite_note-143] while making up the largest foreign-born population in the [Philadelphia metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_metropolitan_area).[^cite_note-144] A Korean community initially settled in the North Philadelphia neighborhood of [Olney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olney,_Philadelphia); however, the primary [Koreatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreatown,_Philadelphia) has subsequently shifted further north, straddling the city's border with adjacent [Cheltenham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheltenham,_Pennsylvania) in [Montgomery County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_County,_Pennsylvania) and [Cherry Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry_Hill,_New_Jersey) in [South Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Jersey). South Philadelphia is home to [Vietnamese-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese-American) in [Little Saigon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Saigon,_Philadelphia) and [Cambodian-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian-Americans) in [Cambodia Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Cambodia#Philadelphia,_Pennsylvania), as well as [Thai-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai-American), [Indonesian-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian-American), and Chinese-American communities.

Philadelphia's [Gay village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_village) near [Washington Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Square_\(Philadelphia\)) is home to a concentration of gay and lesbian-friendly businesses, restaurants, and bars.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Philadelphia)

In a 2014 study by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), 68% of the population of the city identified themselves as [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian).[^cite_note-147] Approximately 41% of Christians in the city and area professed attendance at a variety of churches that could be considered [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant), while 26% professed [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic) beliefs.

The [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) Christian community in Philadelphia is dominated by [mainline Protestant denominations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) including the [Evangelical Lutheran Church in America](248255.md), [United Church of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Church_of_Christ), the [Episcopal Church in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Church_in_the_United_States), [Presbyterian Church (USA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_\(USA\)) and [American Baptist Churches USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Baptist_Churches_USA). One of the most prominent mainline Protestant jurisdictions is the [Episcopal Diocese of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Diocese_of_Pennsylvania). The [African Methodist Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Methodist_Episcopal_Church) was established in Philadelphia. Historically, the city has strong connections to the [Quakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quakers), [Unitarian Universalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitarian_Universalism), and the [Ethical Culture movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethical_Culture_movement), all of which continue to be represented in the city. The Quaker [Friends General Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_General_Conference) is based in Philadelphia. Evangelical Protestants making up less than 15% of the population were also prevalent.

Evangelical Protestant bodies included the [Anglican Church in North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Church_in_North_America), [Lutheran Church—Missouri Synod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_Church%E2%80%94Missouri_Synod), [Presbyterian Church in America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_in_America), and [National Baptist Convention of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Baptist_Convention_of_America). The Catholic community is primarily served by the [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church) [Catholic Archdiocese of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Philadelphia), the [Ukrainian Catholic Archeparchy of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Catholic_Archeparchy_of_Philadelphia), and the [Syro-Malankara Catholic Eparchy of the United States of America and Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syro-Malankara_Catholic_Eparchy_of_the_United_States_of_America_and_Canada), though some [independent Catholic churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Catholic_churches) exist throughout Philadelphia and its suburbs. The Latin Church-based jurisdiction is headquartered in the city, and its see is the [Cathedral Basilica of Saints Peter and Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Basilica_of_Saints_Peter_and_Paul). The Ukrainian Catholic jurisdiction is headquartered in Philadelphia, and is seated at the [Cathedral of the Immaculate Conception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Immaculate_Conception_\(Philadelphia\)).

Less than 1% of Philadelphia's Christians were [Mormons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormons). The remainder of the Christian demographic is spread among smaller Protestant denominations and the [Eastern](10186.md) and [Oriental Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriental_Orthodox) among others. The [Diocese of Eastern Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocese_of_Eastern_Pennsylvania) ([Orthodox Church in America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Church_in_America)) and [Greek Orthodox Archdiocese of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Archdiocese_of_America) ([Ecumenical Patriarchate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecumenical_Patriarchate)) divide the Eastern Orthodox in Philadelphia. The [Russian Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox) [St. Andrew's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Andrew's_Cathedral,_Philadelphia) is in the city. The same study says that other religions collectively compose about 8% of the population, including [Judaism](15624.md), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), [Islam](6037917.md), [Buddhism](3267529.md), and [Sikhism](27964.md).[^cite_note-148] Philadelphia has the fifth-largest [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) population among U.S. cities.[^cite_note-149] The remaining 24% claimed [no religious affiliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion).

The Philadelphia [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metropolitan_Statistical_Areas)'s [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Philadelphia) population was estimated at 206,000 in 2001, which was the sixth-largest in the U.S. at that time.[^cite_note-150] Jewish traders were operating in southeastern Pennsylvania long before [William Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Penn). Jews in Philadelphia took a prominent part in the [War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War). Although the majority of the early Jewish residents were of Portuguese or Spanish descent, some among them had emigrated from Germany and Poland. About the beginning of the 19th century, a number of Jews from the latter countries, finding the services of the [Congregation Mickvé Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_Mickv%C3%A9_Israel) unfamiliar to them, resolved to form a new congregation which would use the ritual to which they had been accustomed.

[African diasporic religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_diasporic_religions) are practiced in some Latino and Hispanic and Caribbean communities in North and West Philadelphia.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152]

### Languages

As of 2010, 79.12% (1,112,441) of Philadelphia residents age 5 and older spoke English at home as a [primary language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_language), while 9.72% (136,688) spoke Spanish, 1.64% (23,075) Chinese, 0.89% (12,499) [Vietnamese](32511.md), 0.77% (10,885) Russian, 0.66% (9,240) French, 0.61% (8,639) [other Asian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Asia), 0.58% (8,217) [African languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_languages), 0.56% (7,933) [Cambodian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian_language) ([Mon-Khmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mon-Khmer)), and Italian was spoken as a [main language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_language) by 0.55% (7,773) of the population over the age of five. In total, 20.88% (293,544) of Philadelphia's population age 5 and older spoke a [mother language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_language) other than English.[^cite_note-153]

### Poverty

Philadelphia is home to many food poverty programs, of which two of the largest are [Philabundance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philabundance) which claims to feed 90,000 people per week.[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] and [Share Food Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Share_Food_Program) which claims to feed 1 million people per month.[^cite_note-158]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Philadelphia)

| **Top publicly traded companies headquartered in Philadelphia** |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Corporation** | **2019** **rank** | **Revenue** **(billions)** |
| [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast) | 32 | 94.5 |
| [Aramark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aramark) | 198 | 15.8 |
| [FMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FMC_Corporation) | 556 | 4.7 |
| [Urban Outfitters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_Outfitters) | 634 | 4.0 |
| [Carpenter Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpenter_Technology) | 940 | 2.2 |
| **Source:** *[Fortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_\(magazine\))*[^cite_note-159] |  |  |

Philadelphia's close geographical and transportation connections to other large metropolitan economies along the [Eastern Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Seaboard) of the United States have been cited as offering a significant competitive advantage for business creation and [entrepreneurship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship).[^cite_note-160] The city is the center of economic activity in both [Pennsylvania](23332.md) and the four-state [Philadelphia metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_metropolitan_area). Five [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies are based in the city. As of 2021, the Philadelphia metropolitan area is estimated to produce a [gross metropolitan product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product) (GMP) of US\$479 billion,[^cite_note-161] an increase from the \$445 billion calculated by the [Bureau of Economic Analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis) for 2017,[^cite_note-162] representing the [ninth-largest U.S. metropolitan economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._metropolitan_areas_by_GDP).

Philadelphia's economic sectors include [financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_services), health care, [biotechnology](4502.md), information technology, trade and transportation, manufacturing, [oil refining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_refining), [food processing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_processing), and tourism. Metropolitan Philadelphia is one of the top five American [venture capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital) hubs, credited to its proximity to New York City's [financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_Manhattan) and [tech and biotechnology ecosystems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tech_companies_in_the_New_York_metropolitan_area).[^cite_note-Maloney-2024-21] Financial activities account for the largest economic sector of the metropolitan area, which is one of the largest [health education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_education) and research centers in the United States. The city's two largest employers are the federal and city governments. Philadelphia's largest private employer is the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania) followed by [Children's Hospital of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_Hospital_of_Philadelphia).[^cite_note-pewtrusts.org-2015-163]

### Finance and corporations

Main article: [List of companies based in the Philadelphia area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_based_in_the_Philadelphia_area)



The [Philadelphia Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Stock_Exchange) building, the nation's first [stock exchange](28168.md), at 1411 [Walnut Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walnut_Street_\(Philadelphia\))

The [Philadelphia Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Stock_Exchange), acquired by [Nasdaq](21559.md) in 2007, is a global leader in [options](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Option_\(finance\)) trading.[^cite_note-Lucas_Downey_and_Somer_Anderson-2022-23] The city is home to the headquarters of [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast), the nation's largest [multinational](214491.md) telecommunications corporation; insurance conglomerates [Cigna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cigna), [Colonial Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_Penn), and [Independence Blue Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Blue_Cross); as well as food services company [Aramark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aramark), chemical makers [FMC Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FMC_Corporation) and [Rohm and Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohm_and_Haas), pharmaceutical companies [GlaxoSmithKline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GlaxoSmithKline), [Amicus Therapeutics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amicus_Therapeutics), [Spark Therapeutics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spark_Therapeutics), [apparel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apparel) retailers [Five Below](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Below) and [Urban Outfitters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_Outfitters) and its subsidiary [Anthropologie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropologie), automotive parts retailer [Pep Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pep_Boys), and stainless steel producer [Carpenter Technology Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpenter_Technology_Corporation).

Other corporate headquarters in the city include [Crown Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Holdings), and [Brandywine Realty Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandywine_Realty_Trust). The headquarters of [Boeing Rotorcraft Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_Rotorcraft_Systems) and its main [rotorcraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotorcraft) factory are in the Philadelphia suburb of [Ridley Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridley_Park); [The Vanguard Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Vanguard_Group) and the U.S. headquarters of [Siemens Healthineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siemens_Healthineers) are headquartered in [Malvern, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malvern,_Pennsylvania), a Philadelphia suburb. Healthcare [conglomerate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conglomerate_\(company\)) [AmerisourceBergen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AmerisourceBergen) and the North American headquarters of furniture retailer [IKEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IKEA) are located in suburban [Conshohocken, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conshohocken,_Pennsylvania). Across the [Delaware River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River) in adjacent [Camden County, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden_County,_New_Jersey), [Campbell's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campbell's) and [Subaru USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subaru) are both headquartered in [Camden, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden,_New_Jersey), and [TD Bank (USA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TD_Bank_\(USA\)) is headquartered in [nearby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_suburb) suburban [Cherry Hill, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry_Hill,_New_Jersey).

### Tech and biotech



[Comcast Technology Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast_Technology_Center) in Center City is the tallest building in Philadelphia.

Philadelphia is a hub for information technology and [biotechnology](4502.md).[^cite_note-164] Philadelphia and Pennsylvania are attracting new [life sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_sciences) ventures.[^cite_note-Archived_June_10-165] As of 2024, the [Philadelphia metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_metropolitan_area) ranks as one of the Big Five U.S. [venture capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital) hubs, enabled by its proximity to both the [entrepreneurial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tech_companies_in_the_New_York_metropolitan_area) and [financial ecosystems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street) of New York City and to the [federal regulatory environment](195149.md) of Washington, D.C.[^cite_note-Maloney-2024-21][^cite_note-Archived_June_10-165]

### Tourism

See also: [List of tourist attractions in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tourist_attractions_in_Philadelphia)

Philadelphia's history attracts many tourists, with the [Independence National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_National_Historical_Park), which includes the [Liberty Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Bell), [Independence Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Hall), and other historic sites, received over 5 million visitors in 2016.[^cite_note-166] The city welcomed 42 million domestic tourists in 2016 who spent \$6.8 billion, generating an estimated \$11 billion in total economic impact in the city and surrounding four counties of Pennsylvania.[^cite_note-167] The annual [Naked Bike Ride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Naked_Bike_Ride) attracts participants from around the United States and internationally to Philadelphia.

### Trade and transportation

Main article: [Transportation in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Philadelphia)

[Philadelphia International Airport](262559.md), a major [Transatlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transatlantic_flight) gateway and transcontinental hub, has undergone a \$900 million [infrastructural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructural) expansion to increase passenger capacity and augment passenger experience, and the airport continues an ongoing capital expenditure program to upgrade facilities and add further amenities.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169] The [Port of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Philadelphia), having experienced the highest percentage growth by [tonnage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit) loaded in 2017 among major U.S. seaports, has doubled its shipping [capacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonnage) to accommodate super-sized [post-Panamax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Panamax) shipping vessels since 2018.[^cite_note-170] Philadelphia's [30th Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30th_Street_Station) is the third-busiest [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) rail hub, following [Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_\(New_York\)) in [Manhattan](45470.md) and [Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Union_Station) in Washington, D.C., transporting over 4 million [inter-city rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-city_rail) passengers annually.\[^cite_note-[www.amtrak.com-171](http://www.amtrak.com-171/)]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Philadelphia)

See also: [List of National Historic Landmarks in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Historic_Landmarks_in_Philadelphia), [List of sites of interest in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sites_of_interest_in_Philadelphia), and [List of tourist attractions in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tourist_attractions_in_Philadelphia)



The [Kimmel Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimmel_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts) at 300 [Broad Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Street_\(Philadelphia\)), home of the [Philadelphia Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Orchestra)

Philadelphia is home to many [national historical sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Site_\(United_States\)) that relate to the founding of the United States. [Independence National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_National_Historical_Park) is the center of these historical landmarks and one of the country's 22 [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](44940.md). [Independence Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Hall), where the [Declaration of Independence](31874.md) was signed, and the [Liberty Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Bell) is housed, are among the city's most popular attractions. Other national historic sites include the homes of [Edgar Allan Poe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar_Allan_Poe_National_Historic_Site) and [Thaddeus Kosciuszko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thaddeus_Kosciuszko_National_Memorial), and early government buildings, including the [First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Bank_of_the_United_States) and the [Second Bank of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Bank_of_the_United_States), [Fort Mifflin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Mifflin), and the [Gloria Dei (Old Swedes') Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Dei_\(Old_Swedes'\)_Church).[^cite_note-National_Park_Service-2017-172] Philadelphia alone has 67 [National Historic Landmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark), the third most of any city in the country.[^cite_note-National_Park_Service-2017-172]

Philadelphia's major science museums include the [Franklin Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Institute), which contains the [Benjamin Franklin National Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin_National_Memorial), the [Academy of Natural Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Natural_Sciences), the [Mütter Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BCtter_Museum), and the [University of Pennsylvania Museum of Archaeology and Anthropology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania_Museum_of_Archaeology_and_Anthropology). History museums include the [National Constitution Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Constitution_Center), the [Museum of the American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_the_American_Revolution), the [Philadelphia History Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_History_Museum), the [National Museum of American Jewish History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_American_Jewish_History), the [African American Museum in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American_Museum_in_Philadelphia), the [Historical Society of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_Society_of_Pennsylvania), the Masonic Library and Museum of Pennsylvania in the [Masonic Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masonic_Temple_\(Philadelphia,_Pennsylvania\)), and the [Eastern State Penitentiary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_State_Penitentiary). Philadelphia is home to the United States's first [zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Zoo)[^cite_note-PhilaZoo-40] and [hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Hospital),[^cite_note-173] as well as [Fairmount Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairmount_Park), one of America's oldest and largest urban parks,[^cite_note-tpl.org-30] founded in 1855.[^cite_note-174]

The city is home to important archival repositories, including the [Library Company of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Library_Company_of_Philadelphia), established in 1731 by [Benjamin Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin) at 1314 [Locust Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Locust_Street),[^cite_note-175] and the [Athenaeum of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athenaeum_of_Philadelphia), founded in 1814.[^cite_note-176] The [Presbyterian Historical Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Historical_Society) is the country's oldest denominational historical society, organized in 1852.[^cite_note-177]

### Arts

See also: [List of museums in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Philadelphia), [List of public art in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_public_art_in_Philadelphia), and [Mummers Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mummers_Parade)



The [Philadelphia Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Museum_of_Art)



*Keys to Community*, a bust of [Benjamin Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin) by [James Peniston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Peniston) at 325 [Arch Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arch_Street) in [Center City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_City,_Philadelphia)

The city is home to multiple art museums, including the [Pennsylvania Academy of the Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Academy_of_the_Fine_Arts) and the [Rodin Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodin_Museum), which holds the largest collection of work by [Auguste Rodin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auguste_Rodin) outside France. The city's largest art museum, the [Philadelphia Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Museum_of_Art), is one of the [largest art museums in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Largest_art_museums_in_the_world). The long flight of [steps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Steps) to the Art Museum's main entrance became famous after the film *[Rocky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky)* (1976).[^cite_note-Weeks-2006-178]

Annual events include the [Philadelphia Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Film_Festival), held annually each October, the [6abc Dunkin' Donuts Thanksgiving Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6abc_Dunkin'_Donuts_Thanksgiving_Day_Parade), the nation's longest-running continuously held [Thanksgiving Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanksgiving_Day) parade, and the [Mummers Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mummers_Parade), the nation's longest continuously held folk parade, which is held every New Year's Day predominantly on [Broad Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Street_\(Philadelphia\)).

Areas such as [South Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Street_\(Philadelphia\)) and the [Old City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_City_\(Philadelphia\)) section of the city have a vibrant night life. The [Avenue of the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenue_of_the_Arts_\(Philadelphia\)) in [Center City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_City,_Philadelphia) contains many restaurants and theaters, such as the [Kimmel Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimmel_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts), home of the [Philadelphia Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Orchestra), and the [Academy of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Music_\(Philadelphia\)), home of [Opera Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera_Philadelphia) and the [Pennsylvania Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Ballet).[^cite_note-Weeks-2006-178] The [Wilma Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilma_Theater_\(Philadelphia\)) and the [Philadelphia Theatre Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Theatre_Company) at the [Suzanne Roberts Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzanne_Roberts_Theatre) produce a variety of new plays.[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] Several blocks to the east are the [Lantern Theater Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lantern_Theater_Company) at [St. Stephens Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Stephen's_Episcopal_Church_\(Philadelphia\));[^cite_note-181] and the [Walnut Street Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walnut_Street_Theatre), a [National Historic Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark) stated to be the oldest and most subscribed-to [theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater_\(structure\)) in the [English-speaking world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English-speaking_world), founded in 1809.[^cite_note-182] In May 2019, the Walnut Street Theatre announced a major expansion to begin in 2020.[^cite_note-183] [New Freedom Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Freedom_Theatre), Pennsylvania's oldest African-American theatre, is located on North Broad Street.

Philadelphia has more [public art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_art) than any other American city.[^cite_note-184] In 1872, the [Association for Public Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_for_Public_Art), formerly the Fairmount Park Art Association, was created as the first private association in the United States dedicated to integrating public art and [urban planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_planning).[^cite_note-185] In 1959, lobbying by the Artists Equity Association helped create the [Percent for Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Percent_for_Art) [ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law), the first for a U.S. city.[^cite_note-186] The program, which has funded more than 200 pieces of public art, is administered by Creative Philadelphia,[^cite_note-188] the city's office of arts and culture.[^cite_note-189] The city has more murals than any other American city, due to the 1984 creation of the Department of Recreation's [Mural Arts Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural_Arts_Program), which seeks to beautify neighborhoods and provide an outlet for [graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti) artists. The program has funded more than 2,800 [murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural) by professional, staff and volunteer artists and educated more than 20,000 youth in underserved neighborhoods throughout Philadelphia.[^cite_note-190]

The city is home to a number of art organizations, including the regional art advocacy nonprofit Philadelphia Tri-State Artists Equity,[^cite_note-191] the [Philadelphia Sketch Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Sketch_Club), one of the country's oldest artists' clubs,[^cite_note-192] and [The Plastic Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Plastic_Club), started by women excluded from the Sketch Club.[^cite_note-193] Many [Old City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_City,_Philadelphia) art galleries stay open late on the [First Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Friday_\(public_event\)) event of each month.[^cite_note-194]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Cuisine of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Philadelphia)



[Pat's Steaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat's_King_of_Steaks) (foreground) and [Geno's Steaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geno's_Steaks) (background) in [South Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Philadelphia)

The city is known for its [hoagies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_sandwich), [stromboli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stromboli_\(food\)), [roast pork sandwich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roast_pork_sandwich), [scrapple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scrapple), [soft pretzels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretzel#Pennsylvania), [water ice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_ice), [Irish potato candy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_potato_candy), [tastykakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tastykake), and the [cheesesteak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheesesteak) sandwich, which was developed by Italian immigrants.[^cite_note-195] The Philadelphia area has many establishments that serve cheesesteaks, including restaurants, [taverns](272204.md), [delicatessens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delicatessen) and pizza parlors.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198] The originator of the thinly sliced steak sandwich in the 1930s, initially without cheese, is [Pat's King of Steaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat's_King_of_Steaks), which faces its rival [Geno's Steaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geno's_Steaks), founded in 1966,[^cite_note-199] across the intersection of 9th Street and Passyunk Avenue in the [Italian Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Market) of [South Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Philadelphia).[^cite_note-200]

[McGillin's Olde Ale House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McGillin's_Olde_Ale_House), opened in 1860 on Drury Street in [Center City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_City,_Philadelphia), is the oldest continuously operated tavern in the city.[^cite_note-201] The [City Tavern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Tavern) is a replica of a historic 18th-century building first opened in 1773, demolished in 1854 after a fire, and rebuilt in 1975 on the same site as part of [Independence National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_National_Historical_Park).[^cite_note-202] The tavern offers authentic 18th-century recipes, served in seven period dining rooms, three wine cellar rooms and an outdoor garden.[^cite_note-203]

The [Reading Terminal Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reading_Terminal_Market) is a historic [food market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marketplace) founded in 1893 in the [Reading Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reading_Terminal) building, a designated National Historic Landmark. The enclosed market is one of the oldest and largest markets in the country, hosting over a hundred merchants offering [Pennsylvania Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Dutch) specialties, [artisan cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artisan_cheese) and meat, locally grown groceries, and specialty and ethnic foods.[^cite_note-204]

### Dialect

Main article: [Philadelphia English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_English)

The traditional Philadelphia accent is considered by some [linguists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linguist) to be the most distinctive accent in North America.[^cite_note-205] The Philadelphia dialect, which is spread throughout the [Philadelphia metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_metropolitan_area) and [South Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Jersey), is part of a larger [Mid-Atlantic American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Atlantic_American_English) family, a designation that also includes the [Baltimore accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_accent). Additionally, it shares many similarities with the [New York accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_accent). Owing to over a century of linguistic data collected by researchers at the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania) under sociolinguist [William Labov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Labov), the Philadelphia dialect has been one of the best-studied forms of [American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_English).[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208] The accent is especially found within the Irish American and Italian American working-class neighborhoods.[^cite_note-209] Philadelphia also has its own unique collection of [neologisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neologism) and slang terms.[^cite_note-210]

### Music

Main article: [Music of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Philadelphia)



The [Curtis Institute of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curtis_Institute_of_Music) at 1726 [Locust Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Locust_Street) in [Center City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_City,_Philadelphia), one of the world's premier [conservatories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_school)

The [Philadelphia Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Orchestra) is generally considered one of the [top five orchestras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Five_\(orchestras\)) in the United States. The orchestra performs at the [Kimmel Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimmel_Center)[^cite_note-211] and has a [summer concert series](236918.md#Venues) at the [Mann Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mann_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts).[^cite_note-212] [Opera Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera_Philadelphia) performs at the nation's oldest continually operating opera house—the [Academy of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Music_\(Philadelphia\)).[^cite_note-Weeks-2006-178] The [Philadelphia Boys Choir & Chorale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Boys_Choir_&_Chorale) has performed its music all over the world.[^cite_note-213] The [Philly Pops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philly_Pops) plays orchestral versions of popular [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz), [swing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_music), [Broadway](725252.md), and [blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues) songs at the Kimmel Center and other venues within the [mid-Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Atlantic_\(United_States\)) region.[^cite_note-214] The [Curtis Institute of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curtis_Institute_of_Music) is one of the world's premier [conservatories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_school) and among the most selective institutes of higher education in the nation.[^cite_note-215]

Philadelphia has played a prominent role in the [music of the United States](171080.md). The culture of [American popular music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_popular_music) has been influenced by significant contributions of Philadelphia area musicians and producers, in both the recording and broadcasting industries. In 1952, the teen dance party program called *Bandstand* premiered on local television, hosted by [Bob Horn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Horn_\(broadcaster\)). The show was renamed *[American Bandstand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Bandstand)* in 1957, when it began national syndication on [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company), hosted by [Dick Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Clark) and produced in Philadelphia until 1964 when it moved to Los Angeles.[^cite_note-216] Promoters marketed youthful musical artists known as [teen idols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teen_idol) to appeal to the young audience. Philadelphia-born singers, including [Frankie Avalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankie_Avalon), [James Darren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Darren), [Eddie Fisher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Fisher), [Fabian Forte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabian_Forte), [Bobby Rydell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Rydell), and [South Philly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Philly)-raised [Chubby Checker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chubby_Checker), topped the music charts, establishing a clean-cut [rock and roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll) image.

[Philly soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philly_soul) music of the late 1960s–1970s is a highly produced version of [soul music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_music) which led to later forms of popular music such as [disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco) and [urban contemporary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_contemporary) [rhythm and blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_blues).[^cite_note-217] On July 13, 1985, [John F. Kennedy Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy_Stadium) was the American venue for the [Live Aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Aid) concert.[^cite_note-218] The city also hosted the [Live 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_8) concert, which attracted about 700,000 people to the [Benjamin Franklin Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin_Parkway) on July 2, 2005.[^cite_note-219]

Notable rock and pop musicians from Philadelphia and its suburbs include [Bill Haley & His Comets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Haley_&_His_Comets), [Nazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazz), [Todd Rundgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Rundgren), [Hall & Oates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hall_&_Oates), [the Hooters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hooters), [Cinderella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinderella_\(band\)), [DJ Jazzy Jeff & the Fresh Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_Jazzy_Jeff_&_the_Fresh_Prince), [Ween](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ween), [Schoolly D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schoolly_D), [Pink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_\(singer\)), [the Roots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roots), [Beanie Sigel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beanie_Sigel), [State Property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Property_\(band\)), [Lisa "Left Eye" Lopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_%22Left_Eye%22_Lopes), [Meek Mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meek_Mill), [Lil Uzi Vert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lil_Uzi_Vert), and others.

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Philadelphia)

See also: [Philadelphia 76ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_76ers), [Philadelphia Flyers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Flyers), [Philadelphia Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Eagles), [Philadelphia Phillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Phillies), and [Philadelphia Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Union)





[Citizens Bank Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizens_Bank_Park) (left), home of the [Phillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phillies) since 2004, and [Lincoln Financial Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Financial_Field) (right), home of the [Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Eagles) since 2003



The [Philadelphia Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Eagles) are awarded the [Vince Lombardi Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vince_Lombardi_Trophy) after winning [Super Bowl LII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LII), on February 4, 2018.



[Xfinity Mobile Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity_Mobile_Arena) in [South Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Philadelphia), home of the two-time [Stanley Cup](66968.md) champion [Philadelphia Flyers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Flyers) of the [National Hockey League](21809.md) (NHL) and three-time champion [Philadelphia 76ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_76ers) of the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) (NBA)



Historic [Boathouse Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boathouse_Row) at night on the [Schuylkill River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schuylkill_River), a symbol of the city's rich history in competitive [rowing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowing)

Philadelphia has one of the nation's richest histories in professional sports, dating back to the mid-19th century. Its first professional sports team, the [Philadelphia Athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Athletics_\(1860%E2%80%931876\)), a professional baseball team, was founded in 1860.[^cite_note-220] The Athletics were initially an [amateur league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Association_of_Base_Ball_Players) team that [turned professional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Association_of_Professional_Base_Ball_Players) in 1871. In 1876, the Athletics joined with seven other teams in founding the [National League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_League_\(baseball\)), now the longest continuously operating league in world sports.[^cite_note-221]

Philadelphia is one of 12 U.S. cities to have teams in [all four major league sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_cities_with_teams_from_four_major_league_sports): the [Philadelphia Phillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Phillies) of [Major League Baseball](38776.md) (MLB), the [Philadelphia Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Eagles) of the [National Football League](21211.md) (NFL), the [Philadelphia Flyers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Flyers) of the [National Hockey League](21809.md) (NHL), and the [Philadelphia 76ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_76ers) of the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) (NBA).[^cite_note-222] The Phillies, formed in 1883 as the Quakers and renamed in 1884,[^cite_note-223] are the oldest team continuously playing under the same name in the same city in the history of American professional sports.[^cite_note-224]

The Philadelphia metro area is also home to the [Philadelphia Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Union) of [Major League Soccer](71802.md) (MLS), plays their home games at [Subaru Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subaru_Park), a [soccer-specific stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soccer-specific_stadium) in [Chester, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chester,_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227]

Philadelphia was the second of eight U.S. cities to win titles in all four major leagues, the MLB, NFL, NHL, and NBA. It won a title in soccer in the now-defunct [North American Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Soccer_League) in 1973. Following the 76ers' victory over the [Los Angeles Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Lakers) in the [1983 NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_NBA_Finals), however, the city's professional teams and their fans endured 25 years without a championship in any professional sport[^cite_note-228] until the Phillies won the [2008 World Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_World_Series), defeating the [Tampa Bay Rays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampa_Bay_Rays).[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] This quarter century without a championship for any Philadelphia sports team is sometimes described as the [Curse of Billy Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curse_of_Billy_Penn), a reference to a 1987 decision that permitted [One Liberty Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Liberty_Place) to become the first building in city history to surpass the height of *[William Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Penn_\(Calder\))*, a statue installed in 1894 atop [City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_City_Hall).[^cite_note-231] In 2004, during the city's championship drought, [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN) placed Philadelphia second on its list of "The Fifteen Most Tortured Sports Cities".[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] The city's sports fans are often both praised and sometimes derided. In 2011, for instance, *[GQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GQ)* magazine named Eagles and Phillies fans the nation's worst professional sports fans, describing them as the "Meanest Fans in America" in summarizing repeated incidents of their drunken behavior and long history of [booing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booing).[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235]

After the Phillies won the 2008 World Series, nine years passed without a championship until the Eagles won their first [Super Bowl](27718.md) following the [2017 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_NFL_season), defeating the [New England Patriots](21719.md) in [Super Bowl LII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LII). Seven seasons later, following the [2024 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_NFL_season), the Eagles won their second Super Bowl, defeating the [Kansas City Chiefs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City_Chiefs) in [Super Bowl LIX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIX).[^cite_note-236]

Major professional sports teams that originated in Philadelphia, which later moved to other cities, include the [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Warriors) basketball team, which played in Philadelphia from 1946 to 1962[^cite_note-237] and the [Oakland Athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland_Athletics) baseball team, which was originally the [Philadelphia Athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Athletics) and played in Philadelphia from 1901 to 1954.[^cite_note-238]

Philadelphia is home to professional, semi-professional, and elite amateur teams in multiple other sports, including [cricket](25675557.md), [rugby league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_league), and [rugby union](25405.md). Major running events in the city include the [Penn Relays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_Relays), the [Philadelphia Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Marathon), and the [Broad Street Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Street_Run). The [Collegiate Rugby Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collegiate_Rugby_Championship) is played annually each June at [Talen Energy Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talen_Energy_Stadium) in Chester.[^cite_note-239]

The city also has a rich history in [rowing](25736.md), which has been popular in Philadelphia since the 18th century.[^cite_note-240] On [Boathouse Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boathouse_Row), a symbol of Philadelphia's rich rowing history, each [Big Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Big_5) member has its own boathouse.[^cite_note-241] Philadelphia hosts numerous local and collegiate rowing clubs and competitions, including the annual [Dad Vail Regatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dad_Vail_Regatta), the largest [intercollegiate rowing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_rowing_\(United_States\)) event in North America with more than 100 participating U.S. and Canadian colleges and universities;[^cite_note-242] the annual [Stotesbury Cup Regatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stotesbury_Cup_Regatta), which is billed as the world's oldest and largest rowing event for high school students;[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] and the [Head of the Schuylkill Regatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_the_Schuylkill_Regatta).[^cite_note-245] The regattas are held on the [Schuylkill River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schuylkill_River) and organized by [Schuylkill Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schuylkill_Navy), an association of area rowing clubs that has produced numerous [Olympic rowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowing_at_the_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-246]

The [Philadelphia Spinners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Spinners) were a professional [ultimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultimate_\(sport\)) team in [Major League Ultimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Ultimate) (MLU) until 2016. The Spinners were one of the original eight teams of the [American Ultimate Disc League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Ultimate_Disc_League) (AUDL), which was founded in 2012. They played at [Franklin Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Field) and won the inaugural AUDL championship and the final MLU championship in 2016.[^cite_note-247] The MLU was suspended indefinitely by its investors in December 2016.[^cite_note-248] As of 2018, the [Philadelphia Phoenix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Phoenix_\(AUDL\)) continue to play in the AUDL.[^cite_note-249]

Philadelphia is home to the [Philadelphia Big 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Big_5), a group of five [NCAA Division I](669931.md) [college basketball](768856.md) programs, including [La Salle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Salle_University), [Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania), [Saint Joseph's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph's_University), [Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_University), and [Villanova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villanova_University) universities.[^cite_note-250] The sixth NCAA Division I school in Philadelphia is [Drexel University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drexel_University). La Salle won the 1954 championship of the [NCAA Division I men's basketball tournament](507399.md).[^cite_note-251] Villanova won the [1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_NCAA_Division_I_men's_basketball_tournament),[^cite_note-252] [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NCAA_Division_I_men's_basketball_tournament),[^cite_note-253] and [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NCAA_Division_I_men's_basketball_tournament)[^cite_note-254] NCAA Division I men's basketball tournaments. Philadelphia was one of the eleven US host cities for the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md).[^cite_note-255]

## Law and government

See also: [Philadelphia City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_City_Hall)



[Old City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_City_Hall_\(Philadelphia\)) at 5th and [Chestnut Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chestnut_Street_\(Philadelphia\)), Philadelphia's town hall from 1800 to 1854



Flag of Philadelphia in front of the [Philadelphia Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Museum_of_Art)

Philadelphia County is a [legal nullity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_nullity). All county functions were assumed by the city in 1952.[^cite_note-Agency_History-2000-256] The city has been coterminous with the county since 1854.[^cite_note-Consolidation_Act_of_1854-58]

Philadelphia's 1952 [Home Rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_Rule) [Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_charter) was written by the City Charter Commission, which was created by the [Pennsylvania General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_General_Assembly) in an act of April 1949, and a city ordinance of June 1949. The existing [city council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_council) received a proposed draft in February 1951, and the electors approved it in an election held in April 1951.[^cite_note-Charter_Commission-1967-257] The first elections under the new Home Rule Charter were held in November 1951, and the newly elected officials took office in January 1952.[^cite_note-Agency_History-2000-256]

The city uses the [strong-mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government#Strong-mayor_form) version of the mayor–council form of government, which is led by one mayor in whom [executive authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_authority) is vested. The mayor has the authority to appoint and dismiss members of all boards and commissions without the approval of the city council. Elected [at-large](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/At-large), the mayor is limited to two consecutive four-year terms, but can run for the position again after an intervening term.[^cite_note-Charter_Commission-1967-257]

### Courts

See also: [District Attorney of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Attorney_of_Philadelphia), [United States Court of Appeals for the Third Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Third_Circuit), and [United States District Court for the Eastern District of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Pennsylvania)

[Philadelphia County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_County) is coterminous with the [First Judicial District of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Judicial_District_of_Pennsylvania). The Philadelphia County [Court of Common Pleas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_courts_of_common_pleas) is the [trial court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_court) of [general jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_jurisdiction) for the city, hearing [felony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felony)-level criminal cases and civil suits above the minimum jurisdictional limit of \$10,000. The court has [appellate jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appellate_jurisdiction) over rulings from the [Municipal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Municipal_Court) and Traffic Courts, and some administrative agencies and boards. The trial division has 70 commissioned judges elected by the voters, along with about one thousand other employees.[^cite_note-258] The court has a family division with 25 judges[^cite_note-259] and an orphans' court with three judges.[^cite_note-260]

As of 2018, the city's [District Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Attorney_of_Philadelphia) is [Larry Krasner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Krasner), a Democrat.[^cite_note-261] The last Republican to hold the office is [Ronald D. Castille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_D._Castille), who left in 1991 and later served as the Chief Justice of the Pennsylvania Supreme Court from 2008 to 2014.[^cite_note-262]

The [Philadelphia Municipal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Municipal_Court) handles traffic cases, misdemeanor and felony criminal cases with maximum incarceration of five years, and civil cases involving \$12,000 or less (\$15,000 in real estate and school tax cases), and all landlord-tenant disputes. The municipal court has 27 judges elected by the voters.[^cite_note-263]

Pennsylvania's three [appellate courts](643.md) also have sittings in Philadelphia. The [Supreme Court of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Pennsylvania), the court of last resort in the state, regularly hears arguments in [Philadelphia City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_City_Hall).[^cite_note-264] The [Superior Court of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Court_of_Pennsylvania) and the [Commonwealth Court of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Court_of_Pennsylvania) also sit in Philadelphia several times a year.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] Judges for these courts are elected at large.[^cite_note-267] The state Supreme Court and Superior Court have deputy [prothonotary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prothonotary) offices in Philadelphia.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269]

Philadelphia is home to the federal [United States District Court for the Eastern District of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Pennsylvania) and the [Court of Appeals for the Third Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Third_Circuit), both of which are housed in the [James A. Byrne United States Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_A._Byrne_United_States_Courthouse).[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271]

### Politics

See also: [List of mayors of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Philadelphia); [Philadelphia City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_City_Council); and [Philadelphia County, Pennsylvania § Politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_County,_Pennsylvania#Politics)



[Musical Fund Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_Fund_Hall) at 808 [Locust Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Locust_Street) hosted the first nominating [Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_National_Convention) in [1856](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1856_Republican_National_Convention)



[Cherelle Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherelle_Parker), ([D](5043544.md)), the 100th and current [Mayor of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Philadelphia)

The current mayor is [Cherelle Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherelle_Parker) who won the election in November 2023.[^cite_note-272] Parker's predecessor, [Jim Kenney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Kenney), served two terms from 2016 to January 2024.[^cite_note-273] Parker is a member of the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Democratic_Party). For over seven decades, since 1952, every [Philadelphia mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Philadelphia#List_of_Mayors) has been a Democrat.

[Philadelphia City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_City_Council) is the legislative branch which consists of ten council members representing individual districts and seven members elected [at-large](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/At-large), all of whom are elected to four-year terms.[^cite_note-274] Democrats are currently the majority and hold 14 seats including nine of the ten districts and five at-large seats. Republicans hold one seat: the [Northeast-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Philadelphia) Tenth District. The [Working Families Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Working_Families_Party) holds two at-large seats making them the council's minority party. The current council president is [Kenyatta Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenyatta_Johnson).[^cite_note-275]

#### Wards and divisions

Philadelphia's political structure consists of a system of wards and divisions. There are 66 wards with 11 to 51 divisions each for a total of 1703 divisions. Each division elects two committee people who are supposed to live within the division boundaries, and committee people select a leader for their ward.[^cite_note-276] Democrats and Republicans elect their own committee people every four years. The committee person's role is to serve as a point of contact between voters and party officials and help get out the vote.[^cite_note-277] Most wards are closed which means the ward leader makes sole endorsement decisions; open wards allow committee people to weigh in on these decisions.[^cite_note-278] There are groups and individuals who are working to elect ward leaders who promote an open ward system.[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]

#### Political party affiliations

2. [Republican](32070.md) (11.3%)

3. [Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_\(United_States\)) (11.0%)

4. [Other Parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_party_\(United_States\)) (2.50%)
